After the revolution the government established a national [education system](1332627.md) that improved adult literacy rates. In 2008, 85% of the adult population was literate, well ahead of the regional average of 62%.[^cite_note-unescolit-207][^cite_note-208] The [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) was 0.749 in 2013, placing Iran in the "high human development" bracket.[^cite_note-UNDP2014-209]

In 2008 annual economic growth of above 5% was necessary to absorb the 750,000 new labor force entrants each year.[^cite_note-jobs-210] In 2020, agriculture was 10% of GDP and employed 16% of the labor force.[^cite_note-CIAWFIR-7] In 2017, the [industrial sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_sector), which includes mining, manufacturing, and construction, was 35% of GDP and employed 35% of the labor force.[^cite_note-CIAWFIR-7] In 2009, mineral products, [notably petroleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Petroleum_of_Iran), accounted for 80% of Iran's export revenues, even though mining employs less than 1% of the labor force.[^cite_note-Ministry_of_Commerce-84]

In 2004 the service sector ranked as the largest contributor to GDP, at 48% of the economy, and employed 44% of workers.[^cite_note-CIA-32] [Women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_Iran) made up 33% of the labor force in 2005.[^cite_note-211] Youth unemployment, aged 15–24, was 29.1% in 2012, resulting in significant [brain drain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran's_brain_drain).[^cite_note-CIA-32][^cite_note-212] In 2016, according to the government, some 40% of the workforce in the [public sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_sector) are either in excess or incompetent.[^cite_note-213]

As of 2025 Iran has a population 92.4M[^cite_note-214], of which 71M are 15 years old or above.[^cite_note-215] Out of a population of 71M, 29,521,716 (67.2% of males and 14% of female) [participated in the labor force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_force_participation) (either as workers or unemployed),[^cite_note-216] or 41% of the population in the ages 15+. The unemployment rate in 2025 was 8.3% (2,450,302 people),[^cite_note-217] and the [unemployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment) rate among the young (ages 15-24) reached to 21.9%.[^cite_note-218] Moreover, as of April 2026 more than a million people lost their jobs due the war with [USA](3434750.md) and [Israel](9282173.md).[^cite_note-219]

### Labor force and the public sector

The Islamic Republic of Iran employs around 8 million individuals, of whom roughly 3 million hold formal positions within the three branches of government, the armed forces, and leadership institutions. These roles encompass bureaucratic staff, civil servants, and uniformed military personnel.[^cite_note-auto1-220]

Beyond the formal government structure, approximately 2.3 million individuals are employed in quasi-governmental organizations, including state-owned enterprises, national banks, municipalities, and the [Islamic Azad University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Azad_University). Additionally, about 2.5 million pensioners receive state stipends, often distributed through the Relief Committee, a government-controlled charitable organization. As a result, nearly one in ten Iranian citizens maintain a regular financial connection to the state.[^cite_note-auto1-220]

### Unemployment

Unemployment in Iran has remained at high single digits officially, but underemployment and joblessness among youth are significant concerns. However, this headline figure masks labor force dropout: over 38 million working-age Iranians (especially women and youth) are economically inactive, and only 25 million were employed, indicating a very low labor participation rate (around 41%).[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222] Youth unemployment is much higher: for ages 18–35 it was around 15%, and for 15–24 year-olds about 20% in 2024–25 (with young women's jobless rate even higher, near 35%).[^cite_note-223]

### Personal income

See also: [Social class in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_class_in_Iran), [Targeted social assistance reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salaries_in_Iran), ["Justice shares"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_subsidy_reform_plan#Objectives), [Mehr housing scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Privatization_in_Iran#%22Justice_shares%22_plan), and [Construction in Iran § Mehr Housing Scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction_in_Iran#Mehr_Housing_Scheme)



Unemployment rate, per-capita income growth and minimum wage, 2000–2009



[GNI per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GNI_\(nominal,_Atlas_method\)_per_capita):\


&#x20; Iran in 2010: \$4,520 nominal. 2012: \$13,000 PPP.

[^cite_note-Mehr1-224]

&#x20; Higher GNI per capita compared to Iran

&#x20; Lower GNI per capita compared to Iran

Iran is classed as a [middle income country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)_per_capita) and has made significant progress in provision of health and education services in the period covered by the [Millennium Development Goals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Development_Goals) (MDGs). In 2010, Iran's average monthly income was about \$500. The [GNI per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNI_per_capita) in 2012 was \$13,000, by [PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity).[^cite_note-CIA-32][^cite_note-Mehr1-224][^cite_note-Aljazeera-225][^cite_note-GNI-226] A minimum national wage applies to each sector of activity as defined by the Supreme Labor Council. In 2009 this was about \$263 per month (\$3,156 per year).[^cite_note-227]

In 2001 some 20% of household consumption was spent on food, 32% on fuel, 12% on health care and 8% on education.[^cite_note-228] In 2015, Iranians [had little personal debt](3961876.md#Debit/credit_cards).[^cite_note-229] In 2007, seventy percent of Iranians owned [their homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction_industry_of_Iran).[^cite_note-ID1-230]

In 2018–2019 the median household income of Iran was 434,905,000 rials (slightl \$3,300), an 18.6% rise from 2017 to 2018, when median household income was about 366,700,000 rials. Adjusted for [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity), Iran's 2017–2018 median income was equivalent to about \$28,647 (2017 conversion factor, private consumption, LCU).[^cite_note-231]

As the average Iranian household size is 3.5, this puts median personal income at around \$8,185.[^cite_note-232] While Iran rates relatively well on income, [median wealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_wealth_per_adult) is very low for its income level, on par with Vietnam or Djibouti, indicating a high level of spending. According to SCI, median household spending in 2018 was 393,227,000 rials, or 90.5% of the median household income of 434,905,000 rials.

After the 1979 revolution, the composition of the middle class in Iran did not change significantly, but its size doubled from about 15% of the population in 1979 to more than 32% in 2000.[^cite_note-LOC8-233] In 2008, the official poverty line in [Tehran](57654.md) for 2008 was \$9,612. The national average poverty line was \$4,932.[^cite_note-234] In 2010, Iran's Department of Statistics announced that 10 million Iranians live under the [absolute poverty line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolute_poverty) and 30 million live under the [relative poverty line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relative_poverty).[^cite_note-235]

[Salaries and wages in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salaries_in_Iran) have not kept up with inflation, causing a steep decline in real purchasing power. The government sets a national minimum wage annually through the Supreme Labor Council.[^cite_note-236] In March 2024, the minimum monthly wage was raised by 35% to 11,107,000 rials (about \\\$185 at the time, including certain benefits).[^cite_note-\:5-237] However, due to rapid rial depreciation, by late 2024 that amount was worth only \~\$137, while the cost of living spiked causing a real decrease of wages.[^cite_note-238] Labor organizations note the minimum wage that once covered roughly half of a family's basic expenses now covers only one-quarter or less of essential costs.[^cite_note-\:5-237]

### Income inequality

According the inequality dataset of the [World Bank](36752.md), in 2002 the [Gini index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gini_coefficient) in Iran was 34.8, a level that is considered to be quite modest. However, closer data analysis reveals significant wealth concentration, with the top 10% of earners holding 52.7% of the national income – a larger share than in the United States or [European countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_countries).[^cite_note-239]

Iran's Gini coefficient fell from \~0.50 in the late 1970s to \~0.40 by the mid-1980s. In the 2000s, inequality fluctuated around a Gini index of 0.38–0.40. In the late 2010s and 2020s, some official data suggested a slight decline in the Gini (possibly due to overall economic contraction hitting higher incomes). The World Bank estimated Iran's Gini index at around 36.0 in 2019, improving to 34.8 in 2022 (on a 0–100 scale where higher is more unequal).[^cite_note-240]

### Poverty

Main articles: [Poverty in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_Iran) and [Squatting in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squatting_in_Iran)

Poverty levels in Iran have risen in recent years amid sanctions and inflation, after some improvements in the 2000s. The Research Center of Iran's Parliament reported that poverty reached 30.1% of the population in 2023, up from about 26% in 2018, meaning around 25–27 million people unable to meet basic needs.[^cite_note-241] Over 5 years, the poverty rate hovered around 30%, indicating persistent hardship. The World Bank estimated that in 2018, only 0.5% of Iranians lived on under \$1.90/day (extreme poverty), but by 2021 the share living under the upper-middle-income country poverty line (\~\$6.85/day) had increased sharply due to inflation.[^cite_note-niacouncil.org-242] By March 2022, Iranian media reported over 32 million people (around 38% of the population) were below the poverty line when considering the ability to afford essential [food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_inflation_in_Iran) and housing, as inflation in food prices outpaced incomes.[^cite_note-243]

[Malnutrition](258979.md) in Iran has been reported as a growing social and economic issue amid the country's ongoing economic crisis. In August 2025, the Iranian newspaper *[Shargh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shargh)*, citing surveys conducted by NGOs and volunteer groups, highlighted severe deficiencies in food access and nutrition, particularly protein intake, across the population.[^cite_note-\:17-244] The study revealed that only 1.7% of households reported daily protein consumption. About 27% of households reported no [protein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protein) intake at all. Among households with temporary employment, over 93% consumed protein less than once a week or not at all. In unemployed households, this figure rose to 95%.[^cite_note-\:17-244]

### Social security, pensions and welfare

See also: [Social Security Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Security_Organization), [State Welfare Organization of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Welfare_Organization_of_Iran), [Civil Servants Pension Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Servants_Pension_Fund), and [Ministry of Welfare and Social Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Welfare_and_Social_Security)

Although Iran does not offer universal social protection, in 1996, the Iranian Center for Statistics estimated that more than 73% of the Iranian population was covered by social security.[^cite_note-Irandev-245] Membership of the social security system for all employees is compulsory.[^cite_note-Labor-246] Social security ensures employee protection against unemployment, disease, old age and occupational accidents.[^cite_note-SSA-247] In 2003, the government began to consolidate its welfare organizations to eliminate redundancy and inefficiency. In 2003 the minimum standard pension was 50% of the worker's earnings but no less than the minimum wage.[^cite_note-SSA-247] Iran spent 22.5% of its 2003 national [budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budget) on [social welfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_welfare) programs of which more than 50% covered pension costs.[^cite_note-CBI2-248] Out of the 15,000 homeless in Iran in 2015, 5,000 were [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_Iran).[^cite_note-249]

### Trade unions

See also: [Trade unions in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_trade_unions#Iran)

Although Iranian workers have a theoretical right to form labor unions, there is no union system in the country. Ostensible worker representation is provided by the [Workers' House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers'_House_of_the_Islamic_Republic_of_Iran), a state-sponsored institution that attempts to challenge some state policies.[^cite_note-250] [Guild unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guild_unions) operate locally in most areas, but are limited largely to issuing credentials and licenses. The right to strike is generally not respected by the state. Since 1979 strikes have often been met by police action.[^cite_note-251]

## Sectors

See also: [Provinces of Iran](448160.md)

### Agriculture, fishing, livestock and forestry

Main articles: [Agriculture in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Iran) and [Forests of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forests_of_Iran)

See also: [Fertile Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertile_Crescent), [Fruit production in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fruit_production_in_Iran), [Persian wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_wine), [Irrigation in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irrigation_in_Iran), [Geography of Iran](64697.md), and [Environmental issues in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_Iran)



Wheat, the most important crop, is grown mainly in the west and northwest whilst rice is the major crop in the Caspian region.

Agriculture contributes 9.5% to the gross domestic product and employs 17% of the labor force.[^cite_note-Nordea-2020-38] About 9% of Iran's land is arable,[^cite_note-252] with the main food-producing areas located in the [Caspian](19653787.md) region and in northwestern valleys. Some northern and western areas support rain-fed agriculture, while others require [irrigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irrigation_in_Iran).[^cite_note-LOC7-253] Primitive farming methods, overworked and under-fertilized soil, poor seed and water scarcity are the principal obstacles to increased production. About one third of total cultivated land is irrigated. Construction of multipurpose [dams and reservoirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_reservoirs_and_dams_in_Iran) along rivers in the [Zagros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zagros) and [Alborz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alborz) mountains have increased the amount of water available for irrigation. Agricultural production is increasing as a result of modernization, mechanization, improvements to crops and livestock as well as land redistribution programs.[^cite_note-www-wds.worldbank.org-254]

Wheat, the most important crop, is grown mainly in the west and northwest. Rice is the major crop in the Caspian region. Other crops include barley, corn, cotton, sugar beets, tea, hemp, tobacco, fruits, potatoes, legumes (beans and lentils), vegetables, fodder plants (alfalfa and clover), almonds, walnuts and spices including [cumin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumin) and [sumac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumac). Iran is the world's largest producer of [saffron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saffron), [pistachios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pistachios), [honey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honey), [berberis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berberis_vulgaris) and [berries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berries) and the second largest [dates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dates_\(fruit\)) producer.[^cite_note-FAO1-255] Meat and dairy products include lamb, goat meat, beef, poultry, milk, eggs, butter, and cheese.

Non-food products include wool, leather, and silk. Forestry products from the northern slopes of the [Alborz Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alborz_Mountains) are economically important. Tree-cutting is strictly controlled by the government, which also runs a [reforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reforestation) program. Rivers drain into the Caspian Sea and are fished for salmon, carp, trout, pike, and sturgeon that produce [caviar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caviar), of which Iran is the largest producer.[^cite_note-www-wds.worldbank.org-254][^cite_note-256]

Since the 1979 revolution, commercial farming has replaced subsistence farming as the dominant mode of agricultural production. By 1997, the gross value reached \$25 billion.[^cite_note-Ministry_of_Commerce-84] Iran is 90% self-sufficient in essential agricultural products, although limited rice production leads to substantial imports. In 2007 Iran reached self-sufficiency in wheat production and for the first time became a net wheat exporter.[^cite_note-257] By 2003, a quarter of Iran's non-oil exports were of agricultural products,[^cite_note-258] including fresh and dried fruits, nuts, animal hides, processed foods, and spices.[^cite_note-Ministry_of_Commerce-84] Iran exported \$736 million worth of [foodstuffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foodstuff) in 2007 and \$1 billion (\~600,000 tonnes) in 2010.[^cite_note-export-259] A total of 12,198 entities are engaged in the [Iranian food industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Iran#Foodstuff), or 12% of all entities in the [industry sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_of_Iran). The sector also employs approximately 328,000 people or 16.1% of the entire industry sector's workforce.[^cite_note-BYI5-260]

The fishing industry in Iran is regulated by the Iran Fisheries Organization (Shilat), focusing on marine capture, aquaculture, and stock enhancement, with key operations in the Persian Gulf and Caspian Sea and major regional fishing hubs in Hormozgan and Chabahar. Exports bring in hundreds of millions of dollars, with tens of thousands of tons of seafood shipped out yearly. [Illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing in the Persian Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal,_unreported_and_unregulated_fishing_in_the_Persian_Gulf) is an acute problem.[^cite_note-261]

[Animal husbandry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_husbandry) in Iran accounts for around 30% of direct agricultural employment, featuring key sectors such as sheep, dairy and poultry production. It traces back thousands of years as a core pillar of the rural and tribal economy. The poultry industry is Iran's second-largest industry next to oil. In 2012, Iran's poultry industry produced approximately 2 billion birds. Poultry production has grown significantly over the past few decades, with output rising from 195,000 tons in 1978 to 2.1 million tons in 2012.[^cite_note-Mirzaei-262]

### Industrial and manufacturing

Main article: [Industry of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_of_Iran)

See also: [IDRO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Development_and_Renovation_Organization_of_Iran), [Iran Electronics Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Electronics_Industries), [Iran Aviation Industries Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Aviation_Industries_Organization), [Iranian Space Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Space_Agency), [Category\:Manufacturing companies of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Manufacturing_companies_of_Iran), and [Category\:Companies of Iran by industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Companies_of_Iran_by_industry)



Iran has a diversified and broad industrial base. In 1998, the United Nations classified Iran's economy as "semi-developed".

Large-scale factory manufacturing began in the 1920s. With the help from the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) and the [United States](3434750.md), Iran's domestic capabilities expanded to include [heavy industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_industries) such as iron and steel, machinery tools, agricultural tractors, communications equipment, vehicles assembly and petrochemical products. During the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War), Iraq bombed many of [Iran's petrochemical plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Petrochemical_Company), damaging the large [oil refinery at Abadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abadan_Refinery) bringing production to a halt. Reconstruction began in 1988 and production resumed in 1993. In spite of the war, many small factories sprang up to produce import-substitution goods and materials needed by the military.[^cite_note-Britannica-263]

Iran's major manufactured products are petrochemicals, steel and copper products. Other important manufactures include automobiles, home and electric appliances, telecommunications equipment, cement and industrial machinery. Iran operates the largest operational population of industrial robots in West Asia.[^cite_note-264] Other products include paper, rubber products, processed foods, [leather products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_leather_industry) and [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals_in_Iran). In 2000, [textile mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_of_Iran#Textiles_and_apparel_industry), using domestic cotton and wool such as *Tehran Patou* and *Iran Termeh* employed around 400,000 people around Tehran, [Isfahan](223149.md) and along the Caspian coast.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266]



Giant Fractionating column manufactured by [Machine Sazi Arak (MSA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machin_Sazi_Arak)

Despite these problems, Iran has progressed in various [scientific and technological fields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Iran), including [petrochemical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Petrochemical_Company), [pharmaceutical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals_in_Iran), [aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Space_Agency), [defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_equipment_manufactured_in_Iran), and [heavy industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_of_Iran). Even in the face of [economic sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions_against_Iran), Iran is emerging as an [industrialized country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrialized_country).[^cite_note-ECO-20100927-267]

#### Handicrafts and textiles

Main articles: [Ministry of Cultural Heritage, Tourism and Handicrafts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Cultural_Heritage,_Tourism_and_Handicrafts); [Iranian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_art); and [Persian rug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_rug)



Persian carpet

Iran has a long tradition of producing [artisanal goods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_art) including [Persian carpets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_carpet), [ceramics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_art#Pottery_and_ceramics), copperware, brassware, glass, leather goods, textiles and wooden artifacts. The country's carpet-weaving tradition dates from pre-Islamic times and remains an important industry contributing substantial amounts to rural incomes. An estimated 1.2 million weavers in Iran produce carpets for domestic and international export markets. More than \$500 million worth of hand-woven carpets are exported each year, accounting for 30% of the 2008 world market.[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269] Around 5.2 million people work in some 250 handicraft fields and contribute 3% of GDP.

#### Automobile manufacturing

Main article: [Iranian automobile industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_automobile_industry)



[Iran Khodro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Khodro) is the largest car manufacturer in the Middle-East. It has established joint-ventures with foreign partners on 4 continents.

As of 2001, 13 public and privately owned automakers within Iran, led by [Iran Khodro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Khodro) and [SAIPA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAIPA) that accounted for 94% of domestic production. Iran Khodro's [Paykan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paykan), replaced by the [Samand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samand) in 2005, is the predominant brand. With 61% of the 2001 market, Khodro was the largest player, whilst Saipa contributed 33% that year. Other car manufacturers, such as the [Bahman Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahman_Group), Kerman Motors, [Kish Khodro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kish_Khodro), Raniran, Traktorsazi, Shahab Khodro and others accounted for the remaining 6%.[^cite_note-270]

These automakers produce a wide range of vehicles including motorbikes, passenger cars such as Saipa's [Tiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saipa_Miniator), vans, mini trucks, medium-sized trucks, heavy trucks, minibuses, large buses and other heavy automobiles used for commercial and private activities in the country. In 2009 Iran ranked fifth in car production growth after China, Taiwan, Romania and India.[^cite_note-271] Iran was the world's [12th biggest automaker in 2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_motor_vehicle_production) and operates a fleet of 11.5 million cars.[^cite_note-Cars-272][^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275] Iran produced 1,395,421 cars in 2010, including 35,901 commercial vehicles.

#### Defense and aerospace

Main articles: [Military of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_Iran) and [Defense industry of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industry_of_Iran)

See also: [List of military equipment manufactured in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_equipment_manufactured_in_Iran) and [Iran Aviation Industries Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Aviation_Industries_Organization)

In 2007 the [International Institute for Strategic Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Strategic_Studies) estimated Iran's defense budget at \$7.31 billion, equivalent to 2.6% of GDP or \$102 per capita, ranking it 25th internationally. The country's [defense industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industry_of_Iran) manufactures many types of arms and equipment. Since 1992, Iran's [Defense Industries Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Industries_Organization) (DIO) has produced its own tanks, armored personnel carriers, [guided missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran's_missile_forces), radar systems, guided missile destroyers, military vessels, submarines and fighter planes.[^cite_note-276] In 2006 Iran exported weapons to 57 countries, including [NATO](21133.md) members, and exports reached \$100 million.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278] It has also developed a sophisticated mobile [air defense system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_defense_system) dubbed as [Bavar 373](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavar_373).[^cite_note-279]

#### Construction

Main pages: [Construction industry in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction_industry_in_Iran), [Iranian architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_architecture), [Urban plans in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_plans_in_Iran), and [Category\:Lists of buildings and structures in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Lists_of_buildings_and_structures_in_Iran)



[HEPCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HEPCO) motor grader (HG180D1) is working on [road construction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road)

Until the early 1950s construction remained in the hands of small domestic companies. Increased income from oil and gas and easy credit triggered a building boom that attracted international construction firms to the country. This growth continued until the mid-1970s when a sharp rise in inflation and a credit squeeze collapsed the boom. The construction industry had revived somewhat by the mid-1980s, although housing shortages and speculation remained serious problems, especially in large urban centers. As of January 2011, the [banking sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_in_Iran), particularly [Bank Maskan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_Maskan), had loaned up to 102 trillion rials (\$10.2 billion) to applicants of [Mehr housing scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction_in_Iran#Mehr_Housing_Scheme).[^cite_note-280] Construction is one of the most important sectors accounting for 20–50% of total private investment in urban areas and was one of the prime investment targets of well-off Iranians.[^cite_note-CBI2-248]

Annual turnover amounted to \$38.4 billion in 2005 and \$32.8 billion in 2011.[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] Because of poor construction quality, many buildings need seismic reinforcement or renovation.[^cite_note-283] Iran has a large [dam building industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_dams_and_reservoirs_in_Iran).[^cite_note-Dam-284]

#### Mining

Main article: [Mining in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_in_Iran)

Mineral production contributed 0.6% of the country's GDP in 2011, a figure that increases to 4% when mining-related industries are included. Gating factors include poor infrastructure, legal barriers, exploration difficulties, and government control over all resources.[^cite_note-Austrade-285] Iran is ranked among the world's 15 major mineral-rich countries.[^cite_note-286]

Although the petroleum industry provides the majority of revenue, about 75% of all mining sector employees work in mines producing minerals other than oil and natural gas.[^cite_note-Ministry_of_Commerce-84] These include coal, iron ore, copper, lead, zinc, chromium, [barite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barite), salt, [gypsum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gypsum), [molybdenum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molybdenum), [strontium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strontium), [silica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silica), uranium, and gold, the latter of which is mainly a by-product of the [Sar Cheshmeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sar_Cheshmeh) copper complex operation.[^cite_note-287] The mine at Sar Cheshmeh in [Kerman Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerman_Province) is home to the world's second largest store of copper.[^cite_note-288] Large iron ore deposits exist in central Iran, near Bafq, [Yazd](451385.md) and Kerman. The government owns 90% of all mines and related industries and is seeking foreign investment.[^cite_note-Austrade-285] The sector accounts for 3% of exports.[^cite_note-Austrade-285]

In 2019, the country was the 2nd largest world producer of [gypsum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gypsum);[^cite_note-289] the 8th largest world producer of [molybdenum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molybdenum);[^cite_note-290] the world's 8th largest producer of [antimony](898.md);[^cite_note-291] the 11th largest world producer of iron ore;[^cite_note-292] the 18th largest world producer of [sulfur](27127.md),[^cite_note-293] in addition to being the 21st largest worldwide producer of [salt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_salt).[^cite_note-294] It was the 13th largest producer in the world of [uranium](31743.md) in 2018.[^cite_note-295]

Iran has recoverable coal reserves of nearly 1.9 billion [short tonnes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Short_ton). By mid-2008, the country produced about 1.3 million short tonnes of coal annually and consumed about 1.5 million short tonnes, making it a net importer.[^cite_note-Iran-296] The country plans to increase hard-coal production to 5 million tons in 2012 from 2 million tons in November 2008.[^cite_note-bloomberg.com-297]

#### Iron and steel



[Mobarakeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobarakeh_Steel_Co.) in [Isfahan](223149.md) is Iran's largest steel mill listed on the [Tehran Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_Stock_Exchange).

[^cite_note-298]

Iran's steel industry is a major global producer, featuring key hubs such as [Mobarakeh Steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobarakeh_Steel_Company) and [Khuzestan Steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khouzestan_Steel_Company). The main steel mills are located in Ahvaz, Isfahan and [Khuzestan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khuzestan). Iran became self-sufficient in steel in 2009.[^cite_note-299] Aluminum and copper production are projected to hit 245,000 and 383,000 tons respectively by March 2009.[^cite_note-bloomberg.com-297][^cite_note-ReferenceA-300] Cement production reached 65 million tons in 2009, exporting to 40 countries.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-300][^cite_note-301]

#### Petrochemicals

See also: [National Petrochemical Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Petrochemical_Company) and [National Iranian Oil Refining and Distribution Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Oil_Refining_and_Distribution_Company)



Iran's refining capacity (2007–2013 est.)

Iran manufactures 60–70% of its equipment domestically, including refineries, oil tankers, drilling rigs, offshore platforms, and exploration instruments.[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305]

Based on a fertilizer plant in [Shiraz](39622.md), the world's largest [ethylene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethylene) unit, in [Asalouyeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asalouyeh), and the completion of other [special economic zone projects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Petrochemical_Company#Special_industrial_economic_zones), Iran's exports in petrochemicals reached \$5.5 billion in 2007, \$9 billion in 2008 and \$7.6 billion during the first ten months of the [Iranian calendar year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_calendar#Solar_Hejri_and_Gregorian_calendars) 2010.[^cite_note-306] [National Petrochemical Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Petrochemical_Company)'s output capacity will increase to over 100 million tpa by 2015 from an estimated 50 million tpa in 2010 thus becoming the world' second largest chemical producer globally after [Dow Chemical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dow_Chemical) with Iran housing some of the world's largest chemical complexes.[^cite_note-NIPC-123]



Giant gas [reactor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_reactor) of Yadavaran gas refinery designed and manufactured by [AzarAb Industries Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AzarAb_Industries)

Major refineries located at [Abadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abadan,_Iran) (site of its first refinery), Kermanshah and Tehran failed to meet domestic demand for gasoline in 2009. Iran's refining industry requires \$15 billion in investment over the period 2007–2012 to become self-sufficient and end gasoline imports.[^cite_note-307] Iran has the fifth cheapest [gasoline prices in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Gasoline_Rationing_Plan_in_Iran#Prices_in_neighboring_countries) leading to [fuel smuggling with neighboring countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Gasoline_Rationing_Plan_in_Iran#Fuel_smuggling).[^cite_note-308]

In November 2019, Iran raised the gasoline prices by 50% and imposed [a strict rationing system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Gasoline_Rationing_Plan_in_Iran) again, as in 2007. The prices per liter gasoline rose to 15,000 rials, where only 60 liters were permitted to private cars for a month. Besides, oil purchase beyond the limit would cost 30,000 rials per liter. Those prices are still well below [target prices set in the subsidy reform plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_subsidy_reform_plan), however. The policy changes came in effect to the [US sanctions](9941859.md), and caused protests across the country.[^cite_note-309] The result of the rationing, a year later, was reduced pollution and wasteful domestic consumption and increase in exports.[^cite_note-310]

#### Oil and gas industry

Main articles: [Petroleum industry in Iran](28205267.md), [Oil reserves in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_reserves_in_Iran), and [Natural gas in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_in_Iran)

See also: [National Iranian Oil Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Oil_Company), [National Iranian Gas Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Gas_Company), [Oil smuggling in Iran](79540260.md), [Category\:Oil fields of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Oil_fields_of_Iran), [Category\:Natural gas in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Natural_gas_in_Iran), [Ministry of Petroleum (Iran)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Petroleum_\(Iran\)), and [Iran Gas Trunkline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Gas_Trunkline)



Countries by [natural gas proven reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_proven_reserves), 2014, based on data from The World Factbook. Iran has the world's [second largest reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_reserves_in_Iran) after Russia

Iran possesses 10% of the world's [proven oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran's_oil_reserves) and 15% of its [gas reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran's_Natural_Gas_Reserves).[^cite_note-EIA-29] Domestic oil and gas along with hydroelectric power facilities provide electical power for its nationwide grids.[^cite_note-EIA-29] Energy wastage in Iran amounts to six or seven billion dollars per year,[^cite_note-payvand5-312] much higher than the international norm.[^cite_note-RFE-103] Iran recycles 28% of its used oil and gas, whereas some other countries reprocess up to 60%.[^cite_note-payvand5-312] In 2008 Iran paid \$84 billion in [subsidies for oil, gas and electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Economic_Reform_Plan#Subsidy_reform_plan).[^cite_note-Subsidy-52] It is the world's third largest consumer of natural gas after United States and Russia.[^cite_note-CIA-32] In 2010 Iran completed its first [nuclear power plant at Bushehr](18522794.md) with Russian assistance.[^cite_note-313]

Iran has been a major oil exporter since 1913. The country's major oil fields lie in the central and southwestern parts of the western [Zagros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zagros) mountains. Oil is also found in northern Iran and in the [Persian Gulf](24761.md). In 1978, Iran was the fourth largest oil producer, [OPEC](166346.md)'s second largest oil producer and second largest exporter.[^cite_note-314] Following the 1979 revolution the new government reduced production. A further decline in production occurred as result of damage to oil facilities during the Iraq-Iran war.[^cite_note-auto-315]

Oil production rose in the late 1980s as pipelines were repaired and new [Persian Gulf](24761.md) fields exploited. By 2004, annual oil production reached 1.4 billion barrels producing a net profit of \$50 billion.[^cite_note-auto-315] Iranian Central Bank data show a declining trend in the share of Iranian exports from oil-products (FY 2006: 84.9%, 2007/2008: 86.5%, 2008/2009: 85.5%, 2009/2010: 79.8%, FY 2010 (first three-quarters): 78.9%).[^cite_note-316] Iranian officials estimate that [Iran's annual oil and gas revenues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Petroleum_\(Iran\)#Revenues_from_crude_oil) could reach \$250 billion by 2015 once [current projects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Pars#South_Pars_development) come on stream.[^cite_note-oil-117]

Pipelines move oil from the fields to the [refineries](195137.md) and to such exporting ports as Abadan, Bandar-e Mashur and [Kharg Island](2210066.md). Since 1997, Iran's state-owned oil and gas industry has entered into major exploration and production agreements with [foreign consortiums](28205267.md#Major_foreign_projects).[^cite_note-LOC6-317][^cite_note-318] In 2008 the [Iranian Oil Bourse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Oil_Bourse) (IOB) was inaugurated in [Kish Island](847277.md).[^cite_note-319] The IOB trades petroleum, petrochemicals and gas in various currencies. Trading is primarily in the [euro](9472.md) and rial along with other major currencies, not including the [US dollar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrodollar). According to the [Petroleum Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Petroleum_\(Iran\)), Iran plans to invest \$500 billion in its oil sector by 2025.[^cite_note-nitc.co.ir-320]

As of August 2025 the crude oil price declined by 15.44% since August 2024.[^cite_note-321] In addition, the [International Energy Agency (IEA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Energy_Agency_Energy_in_Buildings_and_Communities_Programme) says global demand in 2025 is expected to grow by less than 700,000 barrels per day (bpd), while supply is set to rise by 2.5 million bpd— resulting in a surplus of more than 1.8 million bpd and consequently, a further decline in oil price is expected.[^cite_note-\:1-322] The decline of oil price is expected to further aggravate Iran's economic situation since Iran is heavily dependent on Chinese refiners that take advantage on Iran's difficulties to sell oil in the global markets.[^cite_note-\:1-322]

### Services

Despite 1990s efforts towards economic liberalization, government spending, including expenditure by quasi-governmental foundations, remains high. Estimates of service sector spending in Iran are regularly more than two-fifths of GDP, much government-related, including military expenditures, government salaries, and social security disbursements.[^cite_note-CIA-32] Urbanization contributed to service sector growth. Important service industries include public services (including education), commerce, personal services, professional services and tourism.

The total value of transport and communications is expected to rise to \$46 billion in nominal terms by 2013, representing 6.8% of Iran's GDP.[^cite_note-IRD-323] Projections based on 1996 employment figures compiled for the [International Labour Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Labour_Organization) suggest that Iran's transport and communications sector employed 3.4 million people, or 20.5% of the labor force in 2008.[^cite_note-IRD-323]

#### Retail distribution and hospitality

Main articles: [Retail industry in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_of_Iran#Retail_industry) and [Electronic commerce in Iran](64706.md#Electronic_commerce)

See also: [List of bazaars in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_bazaars_in_Iran), [List of shopping malls in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_shopping_malls_in_Iran), [Caravanserai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caravanserai), [Category\:Retailing in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Retailing_in_Iran), and [Category\:Iranian brands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Iranian_brands)

Iran's retail industry consists largely of cooperatives (many of them [government-sponsored](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonyad)), and independent retailers operating in [bazaars](556904.md). The bulk of food sales occur at street markets with prices set by the Chief Statistics Bureau. Iran has 438,478 small grocery retailers.[^cite_note-Euromonitor-324] These are especially popular in cities other than Tehran where the number of hypermarkets and supermarkets is still very limited. More mini-markets and supermarkets are emerging, mostly independent operations. The biggest chainstores are state-owned Etka, [Refah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refah_supermarket), [Shahrvand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahrvand_Chain_Stores_Inc.) and [Hyperstar Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Hyper_Star).[^cite_note-Euromonitor-324] [Electronic commerce in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_commerce_in_Iran) passed the \$1 billion mark in 2009.[^cite_note-325]

In 2012, Iranians spent \$77 billion on food, \$22 billion on clothes and \$18.5 billion on [outward tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Iran#Outward_tourism).[^cite_note-326] In 2015, overall [consumer spending](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_spending) and [disposable income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disposable_income) are projected at \$176.4 billion and \$287 billion respectively.[^cite_note-327]

#### Education

Main articles: [Education in Iran](1332627.md), [Higher Education in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_Education_in_Iran), and [Science and technology in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Iran)

This section is an excerpt from [Education in Iran](1332627.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Education_in_Iran\&action=edit)]



Flag of the Ministry of Education of Iran

Education in [Iran](14653.md) is centralized and divided into [K-12 education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K-12_education) plus [higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education). Elementary and secondary education is supervised by the [Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_\(Iran\)) and higher education is under the supervision of [Ministry of Science, Research and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Science,_Research_and_Technology_\(Iran\)) and [Ministry of Health and Medical Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_and_Medical_Education_\(Iran\)) for medical sciences. As of 2016, around 94% of the Iranian adult population is literate.[^cite_note-Education_in_Iran_\:2-328] This rate increases to 97% among young adults ages between 15 and 24 without any gender consideration.[^cite_note-Education_in_Iran_BY-329] By 2007, Iran had a student-to-[workforce population](#Labor_force) ratio of 10.2%, standing among the countries with the highest ratio in the world.[^cite_note-330]

[Primary school](25058.md) (*Dabestân*, *دبستان*) starts at the age of 6 for a duration of six years. [Junior high school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_high_school) (*Dore-ye Avval-e Dabirestân دوره اول دبیرستان*), also known as middle school, includes three years of Dabirestân from the seventh to the ninth grade. [Senior high school](554992.md) (*Dore-ye Dovom-e Dabirestân*, *دوره دوم دبیرستان*), including the last three years, is mandatory. The student at this level can study theoretical, vocational/technical, or manual fields, each program with its specialties. Ultimately, students are given a [high school diploma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_school_diploma).[^cite_note-Education_in_Iran_wes.org-331] The requirement to enter into higher education is to have a high school diploma, and passing the national university entrance examination, [Iranian University Entrance Exam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_University_Entrance_Exam) (*Konkur کنکور*), which is similar to the French [baccalauréat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baccalaur%C3%A9at) exam (for most of universities and fields of study). Iran suffers from a problem of over education and falsified academic degrees.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333]

[Universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universities), [institutes of technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutes_of_technology), [medical schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_school) and community colleges provide the higher education. Higher education is sectioned by different levels of diplomas: *Fogh-e-Diplom* or *Kārdāni* after two years of higher education, *Kārshenāsi* (also known under the name "license") is delivered after four years of higher education ([bachelor's degree](188874.md)). *Kārshenāsi-ye Arshad* is delivered after two more years of study ([master's degree](170346.md)). After which, another exam allows the candidate to pursue a doctoral program ([Ph.D.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ph.D.)).[^cite_note-Education_in_Iran_wes.org-331]

The Human Rights Measurement Initiative (HRMI)[^cite_note-334] finds that Iran is fulfilling only 91.0% of what it should fulfill for the right to education based on the country's income level.[^cite_note-Education_in_Iran_Iran_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker-335] HRMI breaks down the right to education by examining the rights to both primary and secondary education. While considering Iran's income level, the nation is achieving 99.2% of what should be possible based on its resources (income) for primary education but only 82.9% for secondary education.[^cite_note-Education_in_Iran_Iran_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker-335]

#### Healthcare and pharmaceuticals

Main article: [Healthcare in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Iran)

| IRAN: Healthcare (Source: [EIU](15303116.md))[^cite_note-autogenerated3-336] | 2005 | 2006 | 2007 | 2008 | 2009 | 2010 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Life expectancy, average (years) | 70.0 | 70.3 | 70.6 | 70.9 | 71.1 | 71.4 |
| Healthcare spending (% of GDP) | 4.2 | 4.2 | 4.2 | 4.2 | 4.2 | 4.2 |
| Healthcare spending (\$ per head) | 113 | 132 | 150 | 191 | 223 | 261 |

The constitution entitles Iranians to basic universal health care. By 2008, 73% of Iranians were covered by the voluntary national health insurance system.[^cite_note-autogenerated3-336] Although over 85% of the population use an insurance system to cover their drug expenses, the government heavily subsidizes pharmaceutical production/importation. The total market value of [Iran's health and medical sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_in_Iran) was \$24 billion in 2002 and was forecast to rise to \$50 billion by 2013.[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-BMI4-338] Iran's [pharmaceutical industry](560876.md) is a major domestic sector producing roughly 95% of the country's medicine supply, characterized by high generic [self-sufficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-sufficiency) and localized biopharmaceutical growth. In 2006, 55 pharmaceutical companies in Iran produced 96% (quantitatively) of the medicines for a market worth \$1.2 billion. Major firms such as [Darou Pakhsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darou_Pakhsh), Sobhan Darou and [Cinnagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinnagen) handle large-scale generic formulations alongside advanced biosimilars for immunology, cancers and chronic conditions.[^cite_note-autogenerated3-336][^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340] This figure is projected to increase to \$3.65 billion by 2013.[^cite_note-BMI4-338]

According to Hataminia, S., & Mohammadzadeh Asl, N. (2025), the deteriorating economic indicator of Iran especially, inflation and unemployment rates exert significant negative effects on life expectancy.[^cite_note-341]

#### Real estate and housing

See also: [Iranian National Housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_National_Housing)

#### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Iran)



[Cyrus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyrus_the_Great)' tomb lies in [Pasargadae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasargadae). Iran is home to [30 historic sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_of_World_Heritage_Sites_by_country) which have been inscribed on [UNESCO](21786641.md) World Heritage List.

Although tourism declined significantly during the war with Iraq, it has subsequently recovered. About 1,659,000 foreign tourists visited Iran in 2004 and 2.3 million in 2009 mostly from Asian countries, including the republics of Central Asia, while about 10% came from the European Union and North America.[^cite_note-Encarta:_Iran's_entry-342][^cite_note-Tourism-343]

The most popular tourist destinations are [Mazandaran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazandaran), [Isfahan](223149.md), [Mashhad](406512.md) and [Shiraz](39622.md).[^cite_note-344] In the early 2000s the industry faced serious limitations in infrastructure, communications, industry standards and personnel training.[^cite_note-LOC7-253] Several organized tours from Germany, France and other European countries come to Iran annually to visit archaeological sites and monuments. In 2003 Iran ranked 68th in tourism revenues worldwide.[^cite_note-IRNA5-345] According to [UNESCO](21786641.md) and the deputy head of research for [Iran Travel and Tourism Organization (ITTO)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Heritage,_Handcrafts_and_Tourism_Organization), Iran is rated among the "10 most touristic countries in the world".[^cite_note-IRNA5-345] [Domestic tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_tourism) in Iran is one of the largest in the world.[^cite_note-BYI-346]

#### Banking, finance and insurance

Main articles: [Banking and insurance in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_and_insurance_in_Iran), [Central Bank of Iran](3961876.md), [Islamic Economics Organization of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Economics_Organization_of_Iran), and [Iranian rial](708656.md)

See also: [List of banks in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_banks_in_Iran), [Islamic banking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_banking), [Cryptocurrency in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptocurrency_in_Iran), [Venture capital in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venture_capital_in_Iran), [Shetab Banking System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shetab_Banking_System), [Tehran Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_Stock_Exchange), [Iran Mercantile Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Mercantile_Exchange), [Iran Insurance Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Insurance_Company), and [Central Insurance of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Insurance_of_Iran)

Government loans and credits are available to industrial and agricultural projects, primarily through banks. Iran's unit of currency is the [rial](708656.md) which had an average official exchange rate of 9,326 rials to the U.S. dollar in 2007.[^cite_note-CIA-32] Rials are exchanged on the unofficial market at a higher rate. In 1979, the government nationalized private banks. The restructured banking system replaced interest on loans with handling fees, in accordance with [Islamic law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_banking). This system took effect in the mid-1980s.[^cite_note-LOC5-43]



The [Tehran Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_Stock_Exchange) was one of the world's best performing stock exchanges between 1999 and 2011.

[^cite_note-347]

[^cite_note-businessweek1-348]

The banking system consists of a central bank, the [Bank Markazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_Markazi), which issues currency and oversees all [state and private banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_banks_in_Iran), as well as the [Organization of Islamic Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_of_Islamic_Economics) which acts as a sort of central bank for issuing [Qard al-Hasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qard_al-Hasan), as a parallel system in the banking sector, operating outside the purview of the Central Bank.[^cite_note-\:02-91] It supervises 1200, out of 2500 Islamic loan funds, on behalf of Iran's Central Bank.[^cite_note-349] Several commercial banks have branches throughout the country. Two development banks exist and a housing bank specializes in home mortgages. The government began to [privatize the banking sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Privatization_in_Iran#Banking_and_insurance) in 2001 when licenses were issued to two new privately owned banks.[^cite_note-Financials-350]

State-owned commercial banks predominantly make loans to the state, *[bonyad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonyad)* enterprises, large-scale private firms and four thousand wealthy/connected individuals.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352] While most [Iranians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_peoples) have difficulty obtaining small home loans, 90 individuals secured facilities totaling \$8 billion. In 2009, [Iran's General Inspection Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Inspection_Office_\(Iran\)) announced that [Iranian banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_banks_in_Iran) held some \$38 billion of delinquent loans, with capital of only \$20 billion.

Foreign transactions with Iran amounted to \$150 billion of major contracts between 2000 and 2007, including private and government lines of credit.[^cite_note-353] In 2007, Iran had \$62 billion in assets abroad.[^cite_note-354] In 2010, Iran attracted almost \$11.9 billion from abroad, of which \$3.6 billion was [FDI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Direct_Investment_in_Iran), \$7.4 billion was from international commercial bank loans, and around \$900 million consisted of loans and projects from international development banks.[^cite_note-355]

As of 2010, the [Tehran Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_Stock_Exchange) traded the shares of more than 330 registered companies.[^cite_note-businessweek1-348] Listed companies were valued at \$100 billion in 2011.[^cite_note-356]

Insurance premiums accounted for just under 1% of GDP in 2008,[^cite_note-Iran-296] a figure partly attributable to low average income per head.[^cite_note-Iran-296] Five state-owned insurance firms dominate the market, four of which are active in commercial insurance. The leading player is the Iran Insurance Company, followed by Asia, Alborz and Dana insurances. In 2001/02 third-party liability insurance accounted for 46% of premiums, followed by health insurance (13%), fire insurance (10%) and life insurance (9.9%).[^cite_note-Financials-350]

#### Communications, electronics and IT

Main articles: [Communications in Iran](64706.md), [Telecommunication Company of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunication_Company_of_Iran), [Internet in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_in_Iran), [Media of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Iran), and [Cinema of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Iran)

Broadcast media, including five national radio stations and five national television networks as well as dozens of local radio and television stations are run by the government. In 2008, there were 345 telephone lines and 106 personal computers for every 1,000 residents.[^cite_note-WB2-357] Personal computers for home use became more affordable in the mid-1990s, since when demand for Internet access has increased rapidly. As of 2010, Iran also had the world's third largest number of [bloggers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_blogs) (2010).[^cite_note-358]

In 1998, the Ministry of Post, Telegraph & Telephone, later renamed the [Ministry of Information & Communication Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_and_Communications_Technology_\(Iran\)), began selling Internet accounts to the general public. In 2006, revenues from the Iranian telecom industry were estimated at \$1.2 billion.[^cite_note-359] In 2006, Iran had 1,223 Internet Service Providers (ISPs), all private sector operated.[^cite_note-360] As of 2014, Iran has the largest mobile market in the Middle East, with 83.2 million mobile subscriptions and 8 million smart-phones in 2012.[^cite_note-361]

According to the World Bank, Iran's information and communications technology sector had a 1.4% share of GDP in 2008.[^cite_note-WB2-357] Around 150,000 people work in this sector, including 20,000 in the software industry.[^cite_note-362] 1,200 IT companies were registered in 2002, 200 in software development. In 2014 software exports stood at \$400 million. By the end of 2009, Iran's telecom market was the fourth-largest in the Middle East at \$9.2 billion and was expected to reach \$12.9 billion by 2014 at a compound annual growth rate of 6.9%.

#### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Iran)

See also: [List of roads and highways in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_roads_and_highways_in_Iran), [Tehran Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_Metro), [Islamic Republic of Iran Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Iran_Railways), [Iran Shipping Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Shipping_Lines), [List of airlines of Iran](2449134.md), [List of airports in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Iran), and [List of ports in Iran](70832267.md)



Locomotive production line of [Wagon Pars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagon_Pars) company

Iran has an extensive paved road system linking most towns and all cities. In 2011, the country had 173,000 kilometres (107,000 mi) of roads, of which 73% were paved. In 2007 there were approximately 100 passenger cars for every 1,000 inhabitants.[^cite_note-Cars-272] Trains operated on 11,106 kilometres (6,901 mi) of track.[^cite_note-CIA-32]

Iran's major port of entry is [Bandar-Abbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandar-Abbas) on the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md). After arriving in Iran, imported goods are distributed by trucks and freight trains. The Tehran–Bandar-Abbas railroad, opened in 1995, connects Bandar-Abbas to Central Asia via Tehran and [Mashhad](406512.md). Other major ports include [Bandar Anzali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandar_Anzali) and [Bandar Torkaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandar_Torkaman) on the Caspian Sea and [Khoramshahr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khoramshahr) and [Bandar Imam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandar_Imam) Khomeini on the [Persian Gulf](24761.md).

Dozens of cities have passenger and cargo airports. [Iran Air](168635.md), the national airline, was founded in 1962 and operates domestic and international flights. All large cities have bus transit systems and private companies provide intercity bus services. Tehran, Mashhad, [Shiraz](39622.md), [Tabriz](439791.md), [Ahvaz](599079.md) and Isfahan are constructing underground railways. More than one million people work in the transportation sector, accounting for 9% of 2008 GDP.[^cite_note-363]

In August 2022, President [Ebrahim Raisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebrahim_Raisi)'s cabinet approved a law to import fully assembled foreign cars. His predecessor President [Hassan Rouhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_Rouhani), had outlawed such imports in July 2018 due to [sanctions imposed on Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions_against_Iran). Regular Iranian citizens were unable to buy safe cars at affordable prices.[^cite_note-364]

### Water supply and sanitation

Main article: [Water supply and sanitation in Iran](18304723.md)

This section is an excerpt from [Water supply and sanitation in Iran](18304723.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Iran\&action=edit)]

|  | This article needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(March 2017)* |
| --- | --- |

[Water supply and sanitation in Iran](18304723.md) is overseen by the [Ministry of Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Energy_\(Iran\)), which sets policy and supervises the provision of services. The renewable water per capita in 2025 for each Iranian has decreased to less than 500 cubic meters per year, whereas this figure was previously 1,500 cubic meters.[^cite_note-365]

## International trade

Main article: [Foreign trade of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_trade_of_Iran)

See also: [Ministry of Commerce (Iran)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Commerce_\(Iran\)), [Economic Cooperation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_Cooperation_Organization), [Developing 8 Countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D-8_Organization_for_Economic_Cooperation), [Colombo Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombo_Plan), and [China-Iran trade relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China-Iran_trade_relations)

Treemap of product exports from Iran in 2019.

Exports of Iran by product category (2019)&#x20;

&#x20; Chemical products

&#x20; Transportation

&#x20; Machines

&#x20; Mineral products

&#x20; Plastics and rubbers

&#x20; Metals

&#x20; Foodstuffs

&#x20; Precious metals

&#x20; Textiles

&#x20; Instruments

&#x20; Animal products

&#x20; Vegetable products

&#x20; Paper goods
